The extrema will occur at the intersection points of the boundary constraints with the isoquant lines of $3x + 4y$.\n\nAlternatively, use Lagrange multipliers to find the extrema of $3x + 4y$ subject to each constraint.\n\nFirst, let\'s consider the constraint $x + y = 7.5$. We can express y as $y = 7.5 - x$, substitute into $3x + 4y = 3x + 4(7.5 - x) = 3x + 30 - 4x = 30 - x$. So as x ranges from 0 to 7.5, 30 - x ranges from 30 - 7.5 = 22.5 to 0. So if we consider only this constraint, the values of $3x + 4y$ would range from 0 to 22.5. However, we also have the second constraint $\\frac{4}{x} + \\frac{3}{y} \\leq 2$, which might restrict the feasible region further, potentially lowering the maximum value or raising the minimum value.\n\nSimilarly, for the constraint $\\frac{4}{x} + \\frac{3}{y} = 2$, we can express y in terms of x and then find the intersection points with the line $3x + 4y = K$ for some K. Let me parametrize the second constraint.\n\nFrom $\\frac{4}{x} + \\frac{3}{y} = 2$, we can express y as:\n\n$\\frac{3}{y} = 2 - \\frac{4}{x}$ → $y = \\frac{3}{2 - \\frac{4}{x}} = \\frac{3x}{2x - 4}$, assuming x > 2 to keep $y$ positive. So on this curve, $y = \\frac{3x}{2x - 4}$. Now, we can write $3x + 4y$ as:\n\n$3x + 4\\left(\\frac{3x}{2x - 4}\\right) = 3x + \\frac{12x}{2x - 4} = 3x + \\frac{6x}{x - 2}$.\n\nLet me simplify this expression:\n\n$3x + \\frac{6x}{x - 2} = 3x + \\frac{6x - 12 + 12}{x - 2} = 3x + \\frac{6(x - 2) + 12}{x - 2} = 3x + 6 + \\frac{12}{x - 2} = 3x + 6 + \\frac{12}{x - 2}$.\n\nSo, $K = 3x + 6 + \\frac{12}{x - 2}$ for $x > 2$.\n\nWe need to find the range of K as x varies over $x > 2$ such that y remains positive. Since y = 3x/(2x - 4) must be positive, which is true as numerator and denominator are both positive for x > 2.\n\nSo the domain for x is (2, ∞). Now, we can analyze the function $f(x) = 3x + 6 + \\frac{12}{x - 2}$ for x > 2.\n\nCompute the derivative to find minima/maxima:\n\n$f\'(x) = 3 - \\frac{12}{(x - 2)^2}$. \n\nSet derivative equal to zero:\n\n$3 - \\frac{12}{(x - 2)^2} = 0$ → $(x - 2)^2 = 4$ → $x - 2 = ±2$.\n\nSince x > 2, $x - 2 = 2$ → x = 4.
